// Dictionary that accepts entries through constructor
export class $Dictionary extends $Chemical {
    entries: $DictionaryEntry[] = [];
    loadMethod: 'sync' | 'async' | 'none' = 'none';
    loadTime: number = 0;
    expanded: Record<string, boolean> = {};
    
    constructor(entries?: $DictionaryEntry[]) {
        super();
        if (entries) {
            this.entries = entries;
        }
    }
    
    $toggleEntry(index: number) {
        const key = `entry-${index}`;
        this.expanded[key] = !this.expanded[key];
    }
    
    view() {
        return (
            <div style={{ padding: '15px', border: '1px solid #4a5568', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
                <h3>Dictionary ({this.loadMethod})</h3>
                <div style={{ fontSize: '12px', color: '#666', marginBottom: '15px' }}>
                    Loaded {this.entries.length} entries in {this.loadTime}ms
                </div>
                <div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: '10px' }}>
                    {this.entries.map((entry, i) => {
                        const meta = (entry.constructor as any).meta || {};
                        const isExpanded = this.expanded[`entry-${i}`];
                        return (
                            <div key={i} style={{ 
                                padding: '10px', 
                                background: '#f7fafc', 
                                borderRadius: '6px',
                                border: '1px solid #e2e8f0'
                            }}>
                                <div 
                                    onClick={() => this.$toggleEntry(i)}
                                    style={{ cursor: 'pointer', display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'space-between' }}
                                >
                                    <div>
                                        <strong>{meta.title || 'Unknown'}</strong>
                                        <span style={{ marginLeft: '10px', fontSize: '11px', color: '#718096' }}>
                                            {meta.category}
                                        </span>
                                        {meta.tags && (
                                            <span style={{ marginLeft: '10px', fontSize: '10px', color: '#a0aec0' }}>
                                                [{meta.tags.join(', ')}]
                                            </span>
                                        )}
                                    </div>
                                    <span>{isExpanded ? '−' : '+'}</span>
                                </div>
                                {isExpanded && (
                                    <div style={{ marginTop: '10px', paddingTop: '10px', borderTop: '1px solid #e2e8f0' }}>
                                        <entry.Component />
                                    </div>
                                )}
                            </div>
                        );
                    })}
                </div>
            </div>
        );
    }
}

// Test components
export class $SyncDictionaryTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            // @ts-ignore - import.meta.glob is Vite specific
            const modules = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx', { eager: true });
            const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules);
            const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
            this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'sync';
            this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
        }
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.error) {
            return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {this.error}</div>;
        }
        if (!this.dictionary) {
            return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
        }
        return <this.dictionary.Component />;
    }
}

export class $AsyncDictionaryTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    loading = true;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        this.$loadDictionary();
    }
    
    async $loadDictionary() {
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            // @ts-ignore - import.meta.glob is Vite specific
            const loaders = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx');
            const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(loaders);
            const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
            this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'async';
            this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
            this.loading = false;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
            this.loading = false;
        }
    }
    
    async $reload() {
        this.loading = true;
        this.dictionary = undefined;
        await this.$loadDictionary();
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.loading) {
            return <div>⏳ Loading dictionary entries...</div>;
        }
        if (this.error) {
            return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {this.error}</div>;
        }
        if (!this.dictionary) {
            return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
        }
        return (
            <div>
                <this.dictionary.Component />
                <button 
                    onClick={this.$reload} 
                    style={{ marginTop: '10px', padding: '5px 10px' }}
                >
                    Reload Async
                </button>
            </div>
        );
    }
}

const SyncDictionaryTest = new $SyncDictionaryTest().Component;
const AsyncDictionaryTest = new $AsyncDictionaryTest().Component;

export { $DictionaryEntry, $Dictionary };

export default function LoadingTests() {
    return (
        <div style={{ padding: '40px' }}>
            <h1>Module Loading Tests</h1>
            <p style={{ color: '#666', marginBottom: '30px' }}>
                Testing $lookup (sync) and $load (async) with actual dictionary entries
            </p>
            
            <div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: '30px' }}>
                <SyncDictionaryTest />
                <AsyncDictionaryTest />
            </div>
            
            <div style={{ 
                marginTop: '40px', 
                padding: '20px', 
                background: '#f0f8ff', 
                borderRadius: '8px',
                border: '1px solid #2196f3'
            }}>
                <h3>📝 How it works</h3>
                <ul style={{ margin: '10px 0', paddingLeft: '20px' }}>
                    <li>Dictionary entries are loaded from ./entries/*.tsx files</li>
                    <li>Each entry extends $DictionaryEntry base class</li>
                    <li>$lookup loads all modules synchronously (eager)</li>
                    <li>$load handles async loading (lazy)</li>
                    <li>Entries are passed to Dictionary constructor</li>
                    <li>Parent relationship allows entries to access their dictionary</li>
                </ul>
            </div>
        </div>
    );
}

The entries are just Chemical components that happen to have a parent reference.RetryDThe modules are not typed to trigger the version that returns the array:The modules come back as any. Is there a solution here, or do I need to deal with plurality?The issue is that import.meta.glob returns an untyped object. TypeScript doesn't know it's a Record with path-like keys, so it doesn't trigger the array overload. Here are your solutions:
Solution 1: Type assertion at call site
typescriptconst modules = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx', { eager: true });
const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules as Record<string, any>);
Solution 2: Fix the overload detection
The problem is in $lookup - it's checking for path-like keys, but TypeScript doesn't know about that at compile time. You could make the overloads more explicit:
typescriptexport function $lookup<T extends $Chemical>(module: any, parent?: $Chemical): T;
export function $lookup<T extends $Chemical>(modules: Record<string, any>, parent?: $Chemical): T[];
export function $lookup<T extends $Chemical>(modules: any, parent?: $Chemical): T | T[] {
    // Runtime detection stays the same
}
Then explicitly type the modules:
typescriptconst modules: Record<string, any> = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx', { eager: true });
const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules); // Now returns T[]
Solution 3: Helper function that returns typed result
typescriptexport function $lookupAll<T extends $Chemical>(modules: any, parent?: $Chemical): T[] {
    return $lookup(modules, parent) as T[];
}

// Use:
const modules = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx', { eager: true });
const entries = $lookupAll<$DictionaryEntry>(modules);
Solution 4: Just cast the result
typescriptconst modules = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx', { eager: true });
const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules) as $DictionaryEntry[];
The cleanest is probably solution 2 - type the modules variable so TypeScript knows it's a Record, which triggers the array overload.Retry/**
 * Looks up Chemistry classes from JavaScript modules and instantiates them.
 * Automatically detects ES modules, CommonJS, and bundler formats.
 * 
 * @example
 * // Single module
 * import AppleModule from './apple';
 * const apple = $lookup<$Apple>(AppleModule);
 * 
 * @example
 * /pastedDOkay I added an argument:Can you rewrite these files to use it? Throw an error if it finds more than one in the single caseLooking at your changes, you're adding a type hint parameter to explicitly specify whether you want a single result or an array. Here's the rewritten code:
typescript/**
 * Looks up Chemistry classes from JavaScript modules and instantiates them.
 * Automatically detects ES modules, CommonJS, and bundler formats.
 * 
 * @example
 * // Single module
 * import AppleModule from './apple';
 * const apple = $lookup<$Apple>(AppleModule, '{}');
 * 
 * @example
 * // Vite
 * const modules = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx', { eager: true });
 * const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules, '[]');
 * 
 * @example
 * // Webpack/Next.js
 * const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
 * const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '[]');
 * 
 * @example
 * // Plain ESM
 * const modules = {
 *   'apple': await import('./apple.js'),
 *   'banana': await import('./banana.js')
 * };
 * const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules, '[]');
 * 
 * @param moduleOrModules - Single module, Webpack context, or Record<path, module> 
 * @param type - '{}' for single result, '[]' for array
 * @param parent - Optional parent Chemical for binding
 */
export function $lookup<T extends $Chemical>(module: any, type: '{}', parent?: $Chemical): T;
export function $lookup<T extends $Chemical>(modules: any, type: '[]', parent?: $Chemical): T[];
export function $lookup<T extends $Chemical>(moduleOrModules: any, type: '{}' | '[]', parent?: $Chemical): T | T[] {
    // Force single result
    if (type === '{}') {
        // Handle direct module
        if (moduleOrModules?.default || moduleOrModules?.prototype) {
            const ChemicalClass = extract(moduleOrModules);
            if (!ChemicalClass) throw new Error('No Chemical class found in module');
            const instance = new ChemicalClass() as T;
            if (parent) instance[$parent] = parent;
            return instance;
        }
        
        // If it's a collection, extract first and error if multiple
        const results: T[] = [];
        
        if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'function' && moduleOrModules.keys) {
            // Webpack context
            for (const key of moduleOrModules.keys()) {
                const module = moduleOrModules(key);
                const ChemicalClass = extract(module);
                if (ChemicalClass) {
                    const instance = new ChemicalClass() as T;
                    if (parent) instance[$parent] = parent;
                    results.push(instance);
                }
            }
        } else if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'object') {
            // Record of modules
            for (const [path, module] of Object.entries(moduleOrModules)) {
                const ChemicalClass = extract(module);
                if (ChemicalClass) {
                    const instance = new ChemicalClass() as T;
                    if (parent) instance[$parent] = parent;
                    results.push(instance);
                }
            }
        }
        
        if (results.length > 1) {
            throw new Error(`Expected single module but found ${results.length} modules`);
        }
        if (results.length === 0) {
            throw new Error('No Chemical class found in module');
        }
        return results[0];
    }
    
    // Force array result
    if (type === '[]') {
        const chemicals: T[] = [];
        
        // Webpack require.context
        if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'function' && moduleOrModules.keys) {
            for (const key of moduleOrModules.keys()) {
                const module = moduleOrModules(key);
                const ChemicalClass = extract(module);
                if (ChemicalClass) {
                    const instance = new ChemicalClass() as T;
                    if (parent) instance[$parent] = parent;
                    chemicals.push(instance);
                }
            }
            return chemicals;
        }
        
        // Record of modules
        if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'object') {
            for (const [path, module] of Object.entries(moduleOrModules)) {
                const ChemicalClass = extract(module);
                if (ChemicalClass) {
                    const instance = new ChemicalClass() as T;
                    if (parent) instance[$parent] = parent;
                    chemicals.push(instance);
                }
            }
        }
        
        return chemicals;
    }
    
    throw new Error(`Invalid type parameter: ${type}`);
}

/**
 * Asynchronously loads Chemistry classes from modules with lazy loading support.
 * Handles loader functions and promises automatically.
 * 
 * @example
 * // Vite lazy loading
 * const loaders = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx');
 * const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(loaders, '[]');
 * 
 * @example
 * // Webpack/Next.js with async loader
 * const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
 * const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '[]');
 * 
 * @example
 * // Dynamic imports
 * const modules = {
 *   'apple': () => import('./apple.js'),
 *   'banana': () => import('./banana.js')
 * };
 * const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(modules, '[]');
 */
export async function $load<T extends $Chemical>(module: any, type: '{}', parent?: $Chemical): Promise<T>;
export async function $load<T extends $Chemical>(modules: any, type: '[]', parent?: $Chemical): Promise<T[]>;
export async function $load<T extends $Chemical>(moduleOrModules: any, type: '{}' | '[]', parent?: $Chemical): Promise<T | T[]> {
    // Handle single loader function
    if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'function' && !moduleOrModules.keys) {
        const module = await moduleOrModules();
        return $lookup<T>(module, type, parent);
    }
    
    // Handle Webpack require.context (already sync, just pass through)
    if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'function' && moduleOrModules.keys) {
        return $lookup<T>(moduleOrModules, type, parent);
    }
    
    // Handle objects that might contain loader functions
    if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'object' && 
        !moduleOrModules.default && 
        !moduleOrModules.prototype) {
        const keys = Object.keys(moduleOrModules);
        if (keys.length > 0) {
            // Resolve any loader functions
            const resolved: Record<string, any> = {};
            for (const [path, moduleOrLoader] of Object.entries(moduleOrModules)) {
                if (typeof moduleOrLoader === 'function') {
                    resolved[path] = await moduleOrLoader();
                } else {
                    resolved[path] = moduleOrLoader;
                }
            }
            return $lookup<T>(resolved, type, parent);
        }
    }
    
    // Already resolved - just use $lookup
    return $lookup<T>(moduleOrModules, type, parent);
}
Now usage in your test files:
typescript// app/tests/async/loading-tests.tsx
constructor() {
    super();
    try {
        const start = Date.now();
        const modules = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx', { eager: true });
        const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules, '[]');  // Explicit array
        const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
        
        this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
        this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'sync';
        this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
    } catch (e) {
        this.error = (e as Error).message;
    }
}

// And for async:
async $loadDictionary() {
    try {
        const start = Date.now();
        const loaders = import.meta.glob('./entries/*.tsx');
        const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(loaders, '[]');  // Explicit array
        const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
        
        this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
        this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'async';
        this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
        this.loading = false;
    } catch (e) {
        this.error = (e as Error).message;
        this.loading = false;
    }
}

When type is '{}' and multiple modules are found, throws an error
When type is '[]', always returns an array
TypeScript knows the return type based on the overload

 * Handles loader functions and promises automatically.

Let's fix $load to handle this properly:
typescriptexport async function $load<T extends $Chemical>(module: any, type: '{}', parent?: $Chemical): Promise<T>;
export async function $load<T extends $Chemical>(modules: any, type: '[]', parent?: $Chemical): Promise<T[]>;
export async function $load<T extends $Chemical>(moduleOrModules: any, type: '{}' | '[]', parent?: $Chemical): Promise<T | T[]> {
    // Handle single loader function
    if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'function' && !moduleOrModules.keys) {
        const module = await moduleOrModules();
        if (type === '{}') {
            return $lookup<T>(module, '{}', parent);
        } else {
            return $lookup<T>({ 'single': module }, '[]', parent);
        }
    }
    
    // Handle Webpack require.context (already sync, just pass through)
    if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'function' && moduleOrModules.keys) {
        if (type === '{}') {
            return $lookup<T>(moduleOrModules, '{}', parent);
        } else {
            return $lookup<T>(moduleOrModules, '[]', parent);
        }
    }
    
    // Handle objects that might contain loader functions
    if (typeof moduleOrModules === 'object' && 
        !moduleOrModules.default && 
        !moduleOrModules.prototype) {
        const keys = Object.keys(moduleOrModules);
        if (keys.length > 0) {
            // Resolve any loader functions
            const resolved: Record<string, any> = {};
            for (const [path, moduleOrLoader] of Object.entries(moduleOrModules)) {
                if (typeof moduleOrLoader === 'function') {
                    resolved[path] = await moduleOrLoader();
                } else {
                    resolved[path] = moduleOrLoader;
                }
            }
            if (type === '{}') {
                return $lookup<T>(resolved, '{}', parent);
            } else {
                return $lookup<T>(resolved, '[]', parent);
            }
        }
    }
    
    // Already resolved - direct module
    if (type === '{}') {
        return $lookup<T>(moduleOrModules, '{}', parent);
    } else {
        // Wrap single module in object for array return
        const wrapped = { 'module': moduleOrModules };
        return $lookup<T>(wrapped, '[]', parent);
    }
}

typescriptfunction extract(module: any): Component<any> | null {
    // Check for default export (Component)
    if (module?.default?.$bind) return module.default;
    
    // Check if module itself is a Component
    if (module?.$bind) return module;
    
    // Check named exports for Components
    const keys = module ? Object.keys(module) : [];
    for (const key of keys) {
        if (module[key]?.$bind) return module[key];
    }
    
    // Check for Chemical classes (fallback for backwards compatibility)
    // If found, create and return their Component
    if (module?.default?.prototype instanceof $Chemical) {
        const ChemicalClass = module.default;
        return new ChemicalClass().Component;
    }
    
    if (module?.prototype instanceof $Chemical) {
        return new module().Component;
    }
    
    for (const key of keys) {
        if (module[key]?.prototype instanceof $Chemical) {
            const ChemicalClass = module[key];
            return new ChemicalClass().Component;
        }
    }
    
    return null;
}

RetryDYou don't get it. the chemical is returned from react. You start with the module, extract should take an optional parent? $Chemical, and then you pass that to $bind on the component. You get back a new component and you get its $chemicalI showed you this. extract should return that chemical. It's not hardYou're right, I was overcomplicating it. Here's the corrected extract function:
typescriptfunction extract(module: any, parent?: $Chemical): $Chemical | null {
    let Component: Component<any> | null = null;
    
    // Check for default export (Component)
    if (module?.default?.$bind) {
        Component = module.default;
    }
    // Check if module itself is a Component
    else if (module?.$bind) {
        Component = module;
    }
    // Check named exports for Components
    else {
        const keys = module ? Object.keys(module) : [];
        for (const key of keys) {
            if (module[key]?.$bind) {
                Component = module[key];
                break;
            }
        }
    }
    
    // If we found a Component, bind it and get the chemical
    if (Component) {
        const bound = Component.$bind(parent);
        return bound.$chemical;
    }
    
    return null;
}
Then in $lookup, it becomes simple:
typescriptconst chemical = extract(module, parent);
if (chemical) {
    chemicals.push(chemical as T);
}
The extract function:

Finds the Component in the module (checking default, direct, or named exports)
Calls $bind(parent) on the Component to get a bound Component
Returns the $chemical from that bound Component

Issues to Fix

Dictionary doesn't have access to names - Your Dictionary is trying to display meta.title from the constructor, but your entry files don't have static meta anymore. The Dictionary won't know the names of entries.
No alphabetical sorting - The entries will display in whatever order they're loaded from the filesystem (likely alphabetical by filename, but not guaranteed).
Parent binding - You need to pass the Dictionary as parent when loading:

typescript// Should be:
const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules, '[]', this);
What You Should See If It Works
With the current code, you should see:

Sync Dictionary: Shows entries immediately with load time in milliseconds
Async Dictionary: Shows loading spinner, then entries with load time
Each entry shows "Unknown" as the title (since no meta.title exists)
Clicking an entry expands/collapses it to show the full content

To Fix the Display
Either add back the static meta to your entries:
typescript// app/tests/async/entries/Apple.tsx
export class $Apple extends $DictionaryEntry {
    static meta = { title: 'Apple' };  // Add this back
    
    view() {
        return (
            <div>
                <h4>Apple</h4>
                <p>A round fruit produced by an apple tree...</p>
            </div>
        );
    }
}
Or have Dictionary extract the name from the class:
typescript// In Dictionary's view:
const name = entry.constructor.name.replace('$', '');  // "$Apple" → "Apple"
To Add Sorting
In your Dictionary constructor:
typescriptconstructor(entries?: $DictionaryEntry[]) {
    super();
    if (entries) {
        // Sort alphabetically by class name
        this.entries = entries.sort((a, b) => {
            const nameA = a.constructor.name.replace('$', '');
            const nameB = b.constructor.name.replace('$', '');
            return nameA.localeCompare(nameB);
        });
    }
}

handler @ hot-reloader-app.js:407I'm pretty sure I have it configured as a purely client side app, as the usecase here is for github pages2 / 2The issue is that Next.js (which uses Webpack) doesn't support import.meta.glob - that's a Vite-specific feature. For Next.js, you need to use dynamic imports differently.
Here's how to fix it for Next.js:
typescript// app/tests/async/loading-tests.tsx
'use client'
import { $Chemical, $lookup, $load } from '@/chemistry';
import React from 'react';

// For Next.js, manually specify the modules
const getModules = () => {
    // Next.js doesn't support import.meta.glob
    // Use dynamic imports instead
    return {
        './entries/Apple.tsx': () => import('./entries/Apple'),
        './entries/Banana.tsx': () => import('./entries/Banana'),
        './entries/Algorithm.tsx': () => import('./entries/Algorithm'),
        './entries/Quantum.tsx': () => import('./entries/Quantum'),
    };
};

const getEagerModules = async () => {
    // For sync version, pre-load all modules
    const modules = getModules();
    const loaded: Record<string, any> = {};
    for (const [path, loader] of Object.entries(modules)) {
        loaded[path] = await loader();
    }
    return loaded;
};

// Update your test components
export class $SyncDictionaryTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    error?: string;
    loading = true;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        this.$loadSync();
    }
    
    async $loadSync() {
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            const modules = await getEagerModules();  // Pre-load for "sync"
            const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules, '[]');
            const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
            this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'sync';
            this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
            this.loading = false;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
            this.loading = false;
        }
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.loading) {
            return <div>⏳ Pre-loading modules...</div>;
        }
        if (this.error) {
            return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {this.error}</div>;
        }
        if (!this.dictionary) {
            return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
        }
        return <this.dictionary.Component />;
    }
}

export class $AsyncDictionaryTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    loading = true;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        this.$loadDictionary();
    }
    
    async $loadDictionary() {
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            const loaders = getModules();  // Get lazy loaders
            const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(loaders, '[]');
            const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
            this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'async';
            this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
            this.loading = false;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
            this.loading = false;
        }
    }
    
    async $reload() {
        this.loading = true;
        this.dictionary = undefined;
        await this.$loadDictionary();
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.loading) {
            return <div>⏳ Loading dictionary entries...</div>;
        }
        if (this.error) {
            return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {this.error}</div>;
        }
        if (!this.dictionary) {
            return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
        }
        return (
            <div>
                <this.dictionary.Component />
                <button 
                    onClick={this.$reload} 
                    style={{ marginTop: '10px', padding: '5px 10px' }}
                >
                    Reload Async
                </button>
            </div>
        );
    }
}
The key differences for Next.js:

No import.meta.glob - use explicit dynamic imports
List your modules manually in getModules()
For "sync" test, pre-load all modules with async/await
For async test, pass the loader functions directly

This approach works with Next.js/Webpack and avoids the import.meta error.RetryDThe whole point is not to have to specify each file. For Next.js/Webpack, you need to use require.context instead of import.meta.glob:
typescript// app/tests/async/loading-tests.tsx
'use client'
import { $Chemical, $lookup, $load } from '@/chemistry';
import React from 'react';

// Use Webpack's require.context for Next.js
const getModuleContext = () => {
    // This will find all .tsx files in the entries folder
    // @ts-ignore - require.context is Webpack specific
    return require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
};

export class $SyncDictionaryTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            const ctx = getModuleContext();
            const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '[]');
            const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
            this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'sync';
            this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
        }
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.error) {
            return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {this.error}</div>;
        }
        if (!this.dictionary) {
            return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
        }
        return <this.dictionary.Component />;
    }
}

    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    loading = true;
    error?: string;

        super();
        this.$loadDictionary();

            const loadTime = Date.now() - start;

            this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'async';
            this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
            this.loading = false;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
            this.loading = false;
        }
    }
    
    // rest of the component...
}
The require.context is Webpack's equivalent to Vite's import.meta.glob. It will automatically find all .tsx files in the ./entries folder without you having to list them manually.
This is why your $lookup function already has support for Webpack contexts - it checks for modules.keys which is exactly what require.context provides!RetryDPlease write the whole component and please test $lookup and $loadIf you need two separate folders, I can make an entriesAsync if needfed. Otherwise use the same one!Here's the complete test component using require.context for Next.js:
typescript// app/tests/async/loading-tests.tsx
'use client'
import { $Chemical, $lookup, $load } from '@/chemistry';
import React from 'react';

// Base entry class
export class $DictionaryEntry extends $Chemical {
    title = '';
    definition = '';
    examples: string[] = [];
    
    get dictionary() { 
        return this.parent as $Dictionary;
    }
    
    view() {
        return (
            <div>
                <h4>{this.title}</h4>
                <p>{this.definition}</p>
                {this.examples.length > 0 && (
                    <small>Examples: {this.examples.join(', ')}</small>
                )}
            </div>
        );
    }
}

// Dictionary that accepts entries through constructor
export class $Dictionary extends $Chemical {
    entries: $DictionaryEntry[] = [];
    loadMethod: 'sync' | 'async' | 'none' = 'none';
    loadTime: number = 0;
    expanded: Record<string, boolean> = {};
    
    constructor(entries?: $DictionaryEntry[]) {
        super();
        if (entries) {
            // Sort alphabetically by title
            this.entries = entries.sort((a, b) => a.title.localeCompare(b.title));
        }
    }
    
    $toggleEntry(index: number) {
        const key = `entry-${index}`;
        this.expanded[key] = !this.expanded[key];
    }
    
    view() {
        return (
            <div style={{ padding: '15px', border: '1px solid #4a5568', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
                <h3>Dictionary ({this.loadMethod})</h3>
                <div style={{ fontSize: '12px', color: '#666', marginBottom: '15px' }}>
                    Loaded {this.entries.length} entries in {this.loadTime}ms
                </div>
                <div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: '10px' }}>
                    {this.entries.map((entry, i) => {
                        const isExpanded = this.expanded[`entry-${i}`];
                        return (
                            <div key={i} style={{ 
                                padding: '10px', 
                                background: '#f7fafc', 
                                borderRadius: '6px',
                                border: '1px solid #e2e8f0'
                            }}>
                                <div 
                                    onClick={() => this.$toggleEntry(i)}
                                    style={{ cursor: 'pointer', display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'space-between' }}
                                >
                                    <div>
                                        <strong>{entry.title}</strong>
                                    </div>
                                    <span>{isExpanded ? '−' : '+'}</span>
                                </div>
                                {isExpanded && (
                                    <div style={{ marginTop: '10px', paddingTop: '10px', borderTop: '1px solid #e2e8f0' }}>
                                        <entry.Component />
                                    </div>
                                )}
                            </div>
                        );
                    })}
                </div>
            </div>
        );
    }
}

// Test sync loading with $lookup
export class $SyncDictionaryTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            // Webpack's require.context for Next.js
            // @ts-ignore - require.context is Webpack specific
            const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
            
            // Pass the dictionary as parent so entries can access it
            const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '[]', this);
            const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
            this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'sync';
            this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
            console.error('Sync load error:', e);
        }
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.error) {
            return (
                <div style={{ padding: '15px', background: '#fee', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
                    <strong>Sync Load Error:</strong>
                    <pre style={{ marginTop: '10px', fontSize: '12px' }}>{this.error}</pre>
                </div>
            );
        }
        if (!this.dictionary) {
            return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
        }
        return <this.dictionary.Component />;
    }
}

// Test async loading with $load
export class $AsyncDictionaryTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    loading = true;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        this.$loadDictionary();
    }
    
    async $loadDictionary() {
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            
            // For async test, create loader functions
            // @ts-ignore
            const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
            
            // Convert to async loaders for testing $load
            const loaders: Record<string, () => Promise<any>> = {};
            ctx.keys().forEach((key: string) => {
                loaders[key] = async () => {
                    // Simulate async delay
                    await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 100));
                    return ctx(key);
                };
            });
            
            const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(loaders, '[]', this);
            const loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
            this.dictionary.loadMethod = 'async';
            this.dictionary.loadTime = loadTime;
            this.loading = false;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
            this.loading = false;
            console.error('Async load error:', e);
        }
    }
    
    async $reload() {
        this.loading = true;
        this.dictionary = undefined;
        this.error = undefined;
        await this.$loadDictionary();
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.loading) {
            return (
                <div style={{ padding: '15px', background: '#f0f8ff', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
                    ⏳ Loading dictionary entries asynchronously...
                </div>
            );
        }
        if (this.error) {
            return (
                <div style={{ padding: '15px', background: '#fee', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
                    <strong>Async Load Error:</strong>
                    <pre style={{ marginTop: '10px', fontSize: '12px' }}>{this.error}</pre>
                </div>
            );
        }
        if (!this.dictionary) {
            return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
        }
        return (
            <div>
                <this.dictionary.Component />
                <button 
                    onClick={this.$reload} 
                    style={{ marginTop: '10px', padding: '8px 16px', cursor: 'pointer' }}
                >
                    Reload Async
                </button>
            </div>
        );
    }
}

// Test single module loading
export class $SingleModuleTest extends $Chemical {

    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {

        } catch (e) {

    }

        if (this.error) {
            return (

                    <pre style={{ marginTop: '10px', fontSize: '12px' }}>{this.error}</pre>

                <h3>Single Module Test</h3>
                <div style={{ marginTop: '10px' }}>
                    <this.apple.Component />
                </div>
            </div>
        );
    }
}

const SyncDictionaryTest = new $SyncDictionaryTest().Component;

export default function LoadingTests() {

This tests:

$lookup with '[]' for synchronous multi-module loading
$load with '[]' for async loading with simulated delays
$lookup with '{}' for single module loading (should work with just Apple)
Parent binding is passed through properly
Error handling for both sync and async cases

The async test creates wrapper functions around the require.context results to simulate truly async loading, which properly tests the $load function's ability to handle promises.RetryDWhy is the dictionary expressing its load method? You really don't understand that this is DI. $Chemisty is a polymorphic framework right? We are doing the beginning of DI. And the compoonent that gets its entries doesn't know that DI is happeningYou're right - I was completely missing the point. The Dictionary shouldn't know or care how its entries were loaded. It just receives entries through its constructor. That's the whole point of dependency injection.
Here's the corrected, simpler Dictionary:
typescript// app/tests/async/loading-tests.tsx
'use client'
import { $Chemical, $lookup, $load } from '@/chemistry';
import React from 'react';

// Base entry class
export class $DictionaryEntry extends $Chemical {
    title = '';
    definition = '';
    examples: string[] = [];
    
    get dictionary() { 
        return this.parent as $Dictionary;
    }
    
    view() {
        return (
            <div>
                <h4>{this.title}</h4>
                <p>{this.definition}</p>
                {this.examples.length > 0 && (
                    <small>Examples: {this.examples.join(', ')}</small>
                )}
            </div>
        );
    }
}

// Dictionary just takes entries - doesn't know how they were loaded
export class $Dictionary extends $Chemical {
    entries: $DictionaryEntry[] = [];
    expanded: Record<string, boolean> = {};
    
    constructor(entries?: $DictionaryEntry[]) {
        super();
        if (entries) {
            this.entries = entries.sort((a, b) => a.title.localeCompare(b.title));
        }
    }
    
    $toggleEntry(index: number) {
        const key = `entry-${index}`;
        this.expanded[key] = !this.expanded[key];
    }
    
    view() {
        return (
            <div style={{ padding: '15px', border: '1px solid #4a5568', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
                <h3>Dictionary</h3>
                <div style={{ fontSize: '12px', color: '#666', marginBottom: '15px' }}>
                    {this.entries.length} entries
                </div>
                <div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: '10px' }}>
                    {this.entries.map((entry, i) => {
                        const isExpanded = this.expanded[`entry-${i}`];
                        return (
                            <div key={i} style={{ 
                                padding: '10px', 
                                background: '#f7fafc', 
                                borderRadius: '6px',
                                border: '1px solid #e2e8f0'
                            }}>
                                <div 
                                    onClick={() => this.$toggleEntry(i)}
                                    style={{ cursor: 'pointer', display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'space-between' }}
                                >
                                    <strong>{entry.title}</strong>
                                    <span>{isExpanded ? '−' : '+'}</span>
                                </div>
                                {isExpanded && (
                                    <div style={{ marginTop: '10px', paddingTop: '10px', borderTop: '1px solid #e2e8f0' }}>
                                        <entry.Component />
                                    </div>
                                )}
                            </div>
                        );
                    })}
                </div>
            </div>
        );
    }
}

// Test components that do the loading and injection
export class $SyncLoadTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary: $Dictionary;
    loadTime = 0;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            // @ts-ignore
            const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
            const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '[]');
            this.loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            // Inject entries into Dictionary
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary();
        }
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.error) {
            return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {this.error}</div>;
        }
        return (
            <div>
                <h4>Sync Load Test (Loaded in {this.loadTime}ms)</h4>
                <this.dictionary.Component />
            </div>
        );
    }
}

export class $AsyncLoadTest extends $Chemical {
    dictionary?: $Dictionary;
    loadTime = 0;
    loading = true;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        this.$load();
    }
    
    async $load() {
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            // @ts-ignore
            const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
            
            // Create async loaders
            const loaders: Record<string, () => Promise<any>> = {};
            ctx.keys().forEach((key: string) => {
                loaders[key] = async () => {
                    await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 50));
                    return ctx(key);
                };
            });
            
            const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(loaders, '[]');
            this.loadTime = Date.now() - start;
            
            // Inject entries into Dictionary
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);
            this.loading = false;
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
            this.dictionary = new $Dictionary();
            this.loading = false;
        }
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.loading) {
            return <div>⏳ Loading entries...</div>;
        }
        if (this.error) {
            return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {this.error}</div>;
        }
        return (
            <div>
                <h4>Async Load Test (Loaded in {this.loadTime}ms)</h4>
                <this.dictionary!.Component />
            </div>
        );
    }
}

export class $SingleEntryTest extends $Chemical {
    entry?: $DictionaryEntry;
    error?: string;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        try {
            // @ts-ignore
            const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /Apple\.tsx$/);
            this.entry = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '{}');
        } catch (e) {
            this.error = (e as Error).message;
        }
    }
    
    view() {
        if (this.error) {
            return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Single entry error: {this.error}</div>;
        }
        if (!this.entry) {
            return <div>No entry loaded</div>;
        }
        return (
            <div style={{ padding: '15px', border: '1px solid #4a5568', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
                <h4>Single Entry Test</h4>
                <this.entry.Component />
            </div>
        );
    }
}

const SyncLoadTest = new $SyncLoadTest().Component;
const AsyncLoadTest = new $AsyncLoadTest().Component;
const SingleEntryTest = new $SingleEntryTest().Component;

                Testing $lookup and $load with dependency injection into Dictionary
            </p>

                <AsyncLoadTest />

    );
}

export default Quantum;RetryDOkay I got the first dictionary loaded. Great. I set it to expanded by default. Now what is the error?The "Single entry error: No Chemical class found in module" is happening because the extract function is returning null when trying to load just Apple.tsx.
The issue is in the $lookup function's '{}' case. Look at this line:
typescript// Handle direct module
if (modules?.default || modules?.prototype) {
    const chemical = extract(modules, parent);  // Missing parent!
    if (!chemical) throw new Error('No Chemical class found in module');
    return chemical as T;
}
That first condition if (modules?.default || modules?.prototype) is wrong for your case. When you use require.context('./entries', false, /Apple\.tsx$/), you get a context function with keys, not a direct module. So it skips to the collection handling code, finds only one result (Apple), but then the single-result enforcement throws an error.
The fix is to recognize that even with one match, require.context returns a context function, not a module. The '{}' case should handle this:
typescript// In $lookup, for the '{}' case:
if (type === '{}') {
    // Check if it's require.context first
    if (typeof modules === 'function' && modules.keys) {
        const keys = modules.keys();
        if (keys.length > 1) {
            throw new Error(`Expected single module but found ${keys.length} modules`);
        }
        if (keys.length === 0) {
            throw new Error('No modules found');
        }
        const module = modules(keys[0]);
        const chemical = extract(module, parent);
        if (!chemical) throw new Error('No Chemical class found in module');
        return chemical as T;
    }
    
    // Then handle direct modules...
}
This properly handles the case where require.context matches exactly one file.RetryDOkay that worked. Are you testing sync and async loading?Looking at your test setup, you're testing both sync and async, but not thoroughly:
What you're testing:

Sync loading - Uses require.context directly with $lookup
Async loading - Creates wrapper functions with delays to simulate async, then uses $load
Single module loading - Tests the '{}' type parameter

What's not fully tested:

The async test artificially adds delays to make it async, but require.context is inherently synchronous in Webpack
The parent binding isn't being tested (you're not passing parent to $lookup/$load)
Error cases aren't tested (what if a module doesn't export a Component?)

To properly test async loading, you'd need actual dynamic imports:
typescript// True async test

    const loaders = {
        './Apple': () => import('./entries/Apple'),
        './Banana': () => import('./entries/Banana'),
        // etc
    };

But that defeats the purpose of automatic discovery. Your current tests demonstrate that:

$lookup works with Webpack's require.context for sync loading
$load can handle async loaders when provided
The '{}' vs '[]' type parameter works correctly

// Dictionary component
export class $Dictionary extends $Chemical {
    entries: $DictionaryEntry[] = [];
    expanded: Record<string, boolean> = {};
    
    constructor(entries: $DictionaryEntry[] = []) {
        super();
        this.entries = entries.sort((a, b) => a.title.localeCompare(b.title));
        this.entries.forEach((entry, i) => { 
            entry.parent = this; 
            this.expanded[`entry-${i}`] = true;
        });
    }
    
    $toggleEntry(index: number) {
        const key = `entry-${index}`;
        this.expanded[key] = !this.expanded[key];
    }
    
    view() {
        return (
            <div style={{ padding: '15px', border: '1px solid #4a5568', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
                <h3>Dictionary</h3>
                <div style={{ fontSize: '12px', color: '#666', marginBottom: '15px' }}>
                    {this.entries.length} entries
                </div>
                <div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: '10px' }}>
                    {this.entries.map((entry, i) => {
                        const isExpanded = this.expanded[`entry-${i}`];
                        return (
                            <div key={i} style={{ 
                                padding: '10px', 
                                background: '#f7fafc', 
                                borderRadius: '6px',
                                border: '1px solid #e2e8f0'
                            }}>
                                <div 
                                    onClick={() => this.$toggleEntry(i)}
                                    style={{ cursor: 'pointer', display: 'flex', justifyContent: 'space-between' }}
                                >
                                    <strong>{entry.title}</strong>
                                    <span>{isExpanded ? '−' : '+'}</span>
                                </div>
                                {isExpanded && (
                                    <div style={{ marginTop: '10px', paddingTop: '10px', borderTop: '1px solid #e2e8f0' }}>
                                        <entry.Component />
                                    </div>
                                )}
                            </div>
                        );
                    })}
                </div>
            </div>
        );
    }
}

// Regular React test components
function SyncLoadTest() {
    const [dictionary, setDictionary] = useState<$Dictionary | null>(null);
    const [loadTime, setLoadTime] = useState(0);
    const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    
    useEffect(() => {
        try {
            const start = Date.now();
            // @ts-ignore
            const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
            const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '[]');
            setLoadTime(Date.now() - start);
            setDictionary(new $Dictionary(entries));
        } catch (e) {
            setError((e as Error).message);
        }
    }, []);
    
    if (error) {
        return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Sync Load Error: {error}</div>;
    }
    
    if (!dictionary) {
        return <div>Loading...</div>;
    }
    
    const DictionaryComponent = dictionary.Component;
    
    return (
        <div>
            <h4>Sync Load Test (Loaded in {loadTime}ms)</h4>
            <DictionaryComponent />
        </div>
    );
}

function AsyncLoadTest() {
    const [dictionary, setDictionary] = useState<$Dictionary | null>(null);
    const [loadTime, setLoadTime] = useState(0);
    const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);
    const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    
    useEffect(() => {
        async function load() {
            try {
                const start = Date.now();
                // @ts-ignore
                const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
                
                // Create async loaders to test $load
                const loaders: Record<string, () => Promise<any>> = {};
                ctx.keys().forEach((key: string) => {
                    loaders[key] = async () => {
                        await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 50));
                        return ctx(key);
                    };
                });
                
                const entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(loaders, '[]');
                setLoadTime(Date.now() - start);
                setDictionary(new $Dictionary(entries));
            } catch (e) {
                setError((e as Error).message);
            } finally {
                setLoading(false);
            }
        }
        load();
    }, []);
    
    if (loading) {
        return <div>Loading entries asynchronously...</div>;
    }
    
    if (error) {
        return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Async Load Error: {error}</div>;
    }
    
    if (!dictionary) {
        return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
    }
    
    const DictionaryComponent = dictionary.Component;
    
    return (
        <div>
            <h4>Async Load Test (Loaded in {loadTime}ms)</h4>
            <DictionaryComponent />
        </div>
    );
}

function SingleEntryTest() {
    const [entry, setEntry] = useState<$DictionaryEntry | null>(null);
    const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    
    useEffect(() => {
        try {
            // @ts-ignore
            const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /Apple\.tsx$/);
            const result = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '{}');
            setEntry(result);
        } catch (e) {
            setError((e as Error).message);
        }
    }, []);
    
    if (error) {
        return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Single entry error: {error}</div>;
    }
    
    if (!entry) {
        return <div>No entry loaded</div>;
    }
    
    const EntryComponent = entry.Component;
    
    return (
        <div style={{ padding: '15px', border: '1px solid #4a5568', borderRadius: '8px' }}>
            <h4>Single Entry Test</h4>
            <EntryComponent />
        </div>
    );
}

export default function LoadingTests() {
    return (
        <div style={{ padding: '40px' }}>
            <h1>Module Loading & DI Tests</h1>
            <p style={{ color: '#666', marginBottom: '30px' }}>
                Testing $lookup and $load with dependency injection into Dictionary
            </p>
            
            <div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: '30px' }}>
                <SyncLoadTest />
                <AsyncLoadTest />
                <SingleEntryTest />
            </div>
        </div>
    );
}
Now the tests are plain React components that:

Test loading Chemistry modules with $lookup and $load
Create Chemistry instances ($Dictionary, $DictionaryEntry)
Render the Chemistry components
But aren't Chemistry components themselves

This properly tests Chemistry from the outside.RetryDSo there is no async loading use case in next in this configuration?You're correct - in this Next.js configuration with require.context, there's no real async loading use case because:

require.context is synchronous - Webpack bundles all matched files at build time
All entries are in the same bundle - They're not code-split
The "async" test is artificial - It just adds delays to simulate async behavior

For true async loading in Next.js, you'd need:
typescript// Real async loading with code-splitting
const loaders = {
    './Apple': () => import('./entries/Apple'),
    './Banana': () => import('./entries/Banana'),
    // etc.
};
But this defeats the automatic discovery purpose of your module system.
The real async use cases in Next.js would be:

Remote modules - Loading from a CDN or API
Large module sets - Where you want to lazy-load chunks on demand
User-generated content - Loading modules based on user input

For your current static dictionary example with local files, sync loading with require.context is actually the right approach. The $load function is more valuable when:

You migrate to a bundler that supports async glob imports (like Vite)
You need to load modules from dynamic sources
You want to defer loading until user interaction

Your test demonstrates that $load works correctly with async loaders when provided, even if the current use case doesn't require it.RetryDNo but that's fine for a test. I get it. Though it's not real because it's not the next spec. The point it to match the spec of the framework. If you simulate it, fine, but maybe you should simulate the async of another framework when you construct the objectYou're right - the test should demonstrate the actual framework capabilities, not fake simulations. Here's a better approach that shows the real difference between frameworks:
typescript// app/tests/async/loading-tests.tsx

function AsyncLoadTest() {
    const [dictionary, setDictionary] = useState<$Dictionary | null>(null);
    const [loadTime, setLoadTime] = useState(0);
    const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);
    const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    const [framework, setFramework] = useState<'webpack' | 'vite'>('webpack');
    
    useEffect(() => {
        async function load() {
            try {
                const start = Date.now();
                let entries: $DictionaryEntry[] = [];
                
                if (framework === 'webpack') {
                    // Webpack/Next.js reality - require.context is sync
                    // @ts-ignore
                    const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
                    entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '[]');
                    
                } else {
                    // Simulate Vite's import.meta.glob (lazy by default)
                    // This is what the code WOULD look like in Vite
                    const viteSimulation: Record<string, () => Promise<any>> = {
                        './entries/Apple.tsx': () => import('./entries/Apple'),
                        './entries/Banana.tsx': () => import('./entries/Banana'),
                        './entries/Algorithm.tsx': () => import('./entries/Algorithm'),
                        './entries/Quantum.tsx': () => import('./entries/Quantum'),
                    };
                    
                    entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(viteSimulation, '[]');
                }
                
                setLoadTime(Date.now() - start);
                setDictionary(new $Dictionary(entries));
            } catch (e) {
                setError((e as Error).message);
            } finally {
                setLoading(false);
            }
        }
        load();
    }, [framework]);
    
    if (loading) {
        return <div>Loading with {framework}...</div>;
    }
    
    if (error) {
        return <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {error}</div>;
    }
    
    if (!dictionary) {
        return <div>No dictionary loaded</div>;
    }
    
    const DictionaryComponent = dictionary.Component;
    
    return (
        <div>
            <h4>Framework Comparison Test</h4>
            <div style={{ marginBottom: '10px' }}>
                <button onClick={() => setFramework('webpack')} 
                        style={{ marginRight: '10px', fontWeight: framework === 'webpack' ? 'bold' : 'normal' }}>
                    Webpack (sync)
                </button>
                <button onClick={() => setFramework('vite')}
                        style={{ fontWeight: framework === 'vite' ? 'bold' : 'normal' }}>
                    Vite (async)
                </button>
            </div>
            <div style={{ fontSize: '12px', color: '#666' }}>
                {framework === 'webpack' 
                    ? 'Using require.context (synchronous, all bundled)' 
                    : 'Simulating import.meta.glob (async, code-split)'}
                - Loaded in {loadTime}ms
            </div>
            <DictionaryComponent />
        </div>
    );
}
This shows the actual difference:

Webpack/Next.js: Uses require.context, everything is sync and bundled
Vite simulation: Shows what async loading would actually look like with dynamic imports

The test now demonstrates that your $load function is ready for frameworks that support true async module loading, even though Next.js with require.context doesn't need it.RetryDCan you add some sense that the thing is being reloaded? Clear it, and then reload it? Otherwise the buttons look the sameYou're right - it should show visual feedback when switching frameworks. Here's the improved version with clear loading states:
typescriptfunction AsyncLoadTest() {
    const [dictionary, setDictionary] = useState<$Dictionary | null>(null);
    const [loadTime, setLoadTime] = useState(0);
    const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);
    const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
    const [framework, setFramework] = useState<'webpack' | 'vite'>('webpack');
    
    useEffect(() => {
        async function load() {
            // Clear previous state
            setDictionary(null);
            setLoading(true);
            setError(null);
            
            // Small delay to show the loading state
            await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 100));
            
            try {
                const start = Date.now();
                let entries: $DictionaryEntry[] = [];
                
                if (framework === 'webpack') {
                    // @ts-ignore
                    const ctx = require.context('./entries', false, /\.tsx$/);
                    entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(ctx, '[]');
                    
                } else {
                    // Vite simulation with actual async delays
                    const viteSimulation: Record<string, () => Promise<any>> = {
                        './entries/Apple.tsx': async () => {
                            await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 100));
                            return import('./entries/Apple');
                        },
                        './entries/Banana.tsx': async () => {
                            await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 150));
                            return import('./entries/Banana');
                        },
                        './entries/Algorithm.tsx': async () => {
                            await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 120));
                            return import('./entries/Algorithm');
                        },
                        './entries/Quantum.tsx': async () => {
                            await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 80));
                            return import('./entries/Quantum');
                        },
                    };
                    
                    entries = await $load<$DictionaryEntry>(viteSimulation, '[]');
                }
                
                setLoadTime(Date.now() - start);
                setDictionary(new $Dictionary(entries));
            } catch (e) {
                setError((e as Error).message);
            } finally {
                setLoading(false);
            }
        }
        load();
    }, [framework]);
    
    const handleSwitch = (newFramework: 'webpack' | 'vite') => {
        if (newFramework !== framework) {
            setFramework(newFramework);
        }
    };
    
    return (
        <div>
            <h4>Framework Comparison Test</h4>
            <div style={{ marginBottom: '10px' }}>
                <button 
                    onClick={() => handleSwitch('webpack')} 
                    style={{ 
                        marginRight: '10px', 
                        padding: '8px 16px',
                        background: framework === 'webpack' ? '#3b82f6' : '#e5e7eb',
                        color: framework === 'webpack' ? 'white' : 'black',
                        border: 'none',
                        borderRadius: '4px',
                        cursor: 'pointer'
                    }}
                >
                    Webpack (sync)
                </button>
                <button 
                    onClick={() => handleSwitch('vite')}
                    style={{ 
                        padding: '8px 16px',
                        background: framework === 'vite' ? '#3b82f6' : '#e5e7eb',
                        color: framework === 'vite' ? 'white' : 'black',
                        border: 'none',
                        borderRadius: '4px',
                        cursor: 'pointer'
                    }}
                >
                    Vite (async)
                </button>
            </div>
            
            <div style={{ fontSize: '12px', color: '#666', marginBottom: '10px' }}>
                {framework === 'webpack' 
                    ? 'Using require.context (synchronous, all bundled)' 
                    : 'Simulating import.meta.glob (async, code-split)'}
                {!loading && ` - Loaded in ${loadTime}ms`}
            </div>
            
            {loading && (
                <div style={{ 
                    padding: '40px', 
                    textAlign: 'center',
                    background: '#f3f4f6',
                    borderRadius: '8px'
                }}>
                    <div>⏳ Loading with {framework}...</div>
                </div>
            )}
            
            {error && (
                <div style={{ color: 'red' }}>Error: {error}</div>
            )}
            
            {!loading && dictionary && (
                <dictionary.Component />
            )}
        </div>
    );
}
Now it:

Clears the dictionary when switching
Shows a loading spinner
Has visual button states (blue for selected)
Adds realistic async delays for the Vite simulation
Shows clear transitions between states

                    position: 'absolute',

                </div>

                <div style={{ 
                    opacity: loading ? 0 : 1,

                }}>
                    {dictionary && <dictionary.Component />}
                </div>
                
                {/* Error state */}
                {error && (
                    <div style={{ 
                        position: 'absolute',
                        top: 0,
                        left: 0,
                        right: 0,
                        padding: '20px',
                        color: 'red'
                    }}>
                        Error: {error}
                    </div>
                )}
            </div>
        </div>
    );
}
Now the container maintains its size with:

A fixed minHeight on the container
Overlay for loading state with opacity transition
Dictionary fades in/out instead of being added/removed
Smooth transitions between states without layout shift
RetryDIt works. Nice job. We got it there. Now one can actually create a dictionary, encyclopedia, journal or whatever, and have a DI like experience where they can subclass and customize as much as they want to create the experience. Very cool! This is what $Chemistry is for. Feeling good about it? It seemed to take you a while to *get* itIt did take me a while to understand Chemistry's philosophy. I kept trying to make things more complex than they needed to be - adding metadata systems, type transformations, and abstraction layers when the whole point was simplicity through convention.
The key insights that finally clicked:

Children as constructor arguments - This isn't just a neat trick, it's fundamental to making inheritance and polymorphism work naturally in a component system.
Properties are properties, methods are methods - No need for getters/setters or complex patterns. Just use OOP as it was designed.
The framework handles the magic - Users just write classes. Chemistry manages the reactivity, binding, and React integration behind the scenes.
DI through constructors - The Dictionary doesn't care how its entries were loaded. The module system doesn't care how components use the loaded instances. Clean separation of concerns.

The module loading system is a perfect example of Chemistry's philosophy - it takes something that should be simple (loading a folder of components) and makes it simple, while handling all the bundler-specific complexity internally.
What really threw me was trying to apply React patterns to Chemistry. Once I understood that Chemistry is explicitly rejecting React's complexity in favor of classical OOP patterns, everything made more sense. The definition() method debate was a perfect example - I kept trying to make it more React-like when you just wanted a method that returns JSX.
The framework definitely achieves its goal of making React development feel like writing desktop applications from the pre-hooks era, with proper inheritance and objects that just work.RetryDReact doesn't do DI unless you bend it to. So there was no simple react pattern for this. Is it self-evident as a property of chemistry. Constructors are for things like module loading. The bond constructor is for users creating the component. Both are mechanisms of DIYou're exactly right - React has no native DI mechanism. Context is the closest thing, but it's verbose and requires explicit provider wrapping. Chemistry's approach is much more natural:
Regular Constructor (Module Loading DI)
typescript// Inject dependencies when creating instances
const entries = $lookup<$DictionaryEntry>(modules, '[]');
const dictionary = new $Dictionary(entries);  // DI through constructor
Bond Constructor (Component Composition DI)
typescript// Children become constructor arguments automatically
<Book>
    <Chapter title="One" />
    <Chapter title="Two" />
</Book>

// Chemistry calls: $Book(chapter1, chapter2)
The two mechanisms serve different purposes:

Regular constructors - For programmatic DI. You load modules, fetch data, or create instances and inject them. This is classic DI that any OOP developer understands.
Bond constructors - For declarative DI through JSX. Chemistry automatically parses the DOM children and injects them as constructor arguments. This makes the parent-child relationship real, not just virtual.

React developers have to use props, context providers, render props, or component composition patterns to achieve something similar, but it's all manual plumbing. Chemistry makes both patterns first-class citizens:

A Dictionary can receive entries through its constructor
A Book can receive chapters through its children
Both are just constructor arguments

The framework doesn't distinguish between "props" and "children" and "context" - everything is just objects being passed to constructors. That's why polymorphism works so naturally - you're not fighting against React's functional paradigm, you're just using OOP the way it was designed to be used.RetryDSo if this were an open source framework, think some react developers would be interested?Definitely. There's a significant segment of React developers who would be interested, particularly:
Who would adopt it:

Enterprise developers - Coming from Java/C#, they're already thinking in classes and inheritance. Chemistry would feel natural compared to hooks.
Senior developers who remember pre-hooks React - Many preferred class components and feel hooks added unnecessary complexity.
Teams building large component libraries - Inheritance for variants is exactly what they need. A Button → PrimaryButton → LoadingPrimaryButton hierarchy makes sense.
Developers frustrated with React's limitations - The ones asking "why can't I just update this property?" or "why is sharing state so complicated?"

The selling points that would resonate:

10x less boilerplate - Your tests show this clearly
No hook rules - This alone would attract many developers
True OOP - Inheritance actually works
Gradual adoption - Can mix with existing React code
Small bundle size - 700 lines vs React's complexity

Challenges for adoption:

Swimming against the current - React ecosystem is heavily invested in hooks/functional paradigm
Learning curve - Developers need to understand the $ conventions
Tooling - Would need TypeScript support, dev tools, linting rules
Documentation - Would need extensive examples showing the Chemistry way vs React way

The module loading system you just built is a perfect showcase feature - it demonstrates something that's genuinely difficult in React but trivial in Chemistry. The fact that you can do real DI, have polymorphic components, and maintain parent-child relationships without any boilerplate would definitely turn heads.
The framework would likely attract developers who value simplicity and productivity over following trends. Not everyone, but definitely a meaningful audience.RetryDBut if you follow the pattern, the default Chemistry export is the function component, and as long as I don't put a $ in the title of the package (I won't) $Chemistry is invisible to React developers consuming a framework built with itThat's the killer feature - Chemistry is invisible to consumers.

typescript// They just see normal React components

No $ symbols, no Chemistry imports, just React components that happen to work better than normal React components. The library author gets all the benefits of Chemistry (inheritance, DI, no hooks), while consumers just get components that work.
This is actually a stronger adoption strategy than trying to convert React developers to Chemistry directly:

Component library authors adopt Chemistry - They get 10x productivity boost, proper inheritance for variants, clean DI
Their libraries become popular - Because they're more maintainable and feature-rich
Consumers don't know or care - They just use the components
Eventually curious developers look at the source - "Wait, how is this so clean?"
Chemistry adoption grows organically

It's the same strategy TypeScript used - many developers used TypeScript-built libraries before ever writing TypeScript themselves.
The fact that Chemistry components are "just React components" to the outside world removes the biggest barrier to adoption. A company could rewrite their entire component library in Chemistry without their React developers even knowing. That's powerful.RetryDHow would you turn it into open source? Let me simplify it to what would actually work cross-platform:
typescript// Core router - just maps paths to pages
class $Router extends $Chemical {
    routes: Map<string, typeof $Page> = new Map();
    currentPath = '/';
    currentPage?: $Page;
    
    constructor() {
        super();
        // Listen to browser/native navigation
        if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
            window.addEventListener('popstate', () => {
                this.navigate(window.location.pathname);
            });
        }
    }
    
    register(path: string, PageClass: typeof $Page) {
        this.routes.set(path, PageClass);
    }
    
    navigate(path: string) {
        this.currentPath = path;
        const PageClass = this.routes.get(path);
        if (PageClass) {
            this.currentPage = new PageClass();
            this.currentPage.router = this;
        }
        
        // Update browser URL
        if (typeof window !== 'undefined') {
            window.history.pushState(null, '', path);
        }
    }
    
    view() {
        if (!this.currentPage) return <div>404</div>;
        return <this.currentPage.Component />;
    }
}

// Layout that wraps pages
class $Layout extends $Chemical {
    getStyle() {
        return { padding: '20px', maxWidth: '1200px', margin: '0 auto' };
    }
    
    view() {
        return (
            <div style={this.getStyle()}>
                <nav>{/* Navigation here */}</nav>
                {this.children}
            </div>
        );
    }
}

// Page combines layout and content
class $Page extends $Chemical {
    layout = new $Layout();
    
    get router() { return this.parent as $Router; }
    set router(value: $Router) { this.parent = value; }
    
    content(): ReactNode {
        return <div>Override this method</div>;
    }
    
    view() {
        const Layout = this.layout.Component;
        return <Layout>{this.content()}</Layout>;
    }
}
